## Session 129 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `serverErrorFromCatch` migration in `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` (1 site)

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1 (2nd List-1 pick in a row). The List 1 surface was already closed for the static-literal `serverErrorFromCatch` form in session 125, but a pre-audit revealed one overlooked site: `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:254-256` had a `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"\`)` pair that the scanner intentionally does NOT match (the dynamic-message rule, session 98 P-4 — the message is a template-literal-with-variable-interpolation, not a static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` form). The migration is belt-and-suspenders, not a scanner-enforced invariant, but it removes the only `logApiError + return serverError` pair in the List 1 surface that's still using the inline form.

### Carryover from the previous cron session

This session also finalised the **session 128 cron carryover** that was sitting uncommitted at the start of the session — the `serverErrorFromError` helper promotion (the dynamic-message sister-helper of `serverErrorFromCatch`) plus the 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE handlers) plus 2 new test files (`server-error-from-error.test.ts` + `server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts`, 19 tests total). The carryover work was List 2 territory (`api/cron/hardware` is a Cron page, not a List 1 page) but the helper itself is reusable anywhere — promoting it in a session-128 cron job and finishing it in session 129 keeps the helper's home module consistent with `serverErrorFromCatch` (both live in `@/lib/api-logger`). The full carryover write-up is below.

### 1. `serverErrorFromCatch` migration in `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` (1 site)

The route had one pre-`serverErrorFromCatch` 2-line block:

```ts
} catch (error) {
  logApiError("GET /api/sessions/[id]", "reading session " + sanitizedId, error);
  return serverError(`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"`);
}
```

→ collapsed to:

```ts
} catch (error) {
  return serverErrorFromCatch(
    "GET /api/sessions/[id]",
    "reading session " + sanitizedId,
    error,
    `Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"`,
  );
}
```

The `serverError` import was dropped from `@/lib/api-response` (no longer used after the migration) and `serverErrorFromCatch` was added to the existing `@/lib/api-logger` import. The neighbouring `logApiError` calls at lines 127 and 221 (Hermes state.db read + JSONL parsing) are NOT candidates — they're inner-catch blocks that just log and fall through, no `return` involved, so they correctly use the bare `logApiError` form.

**Why the scanner never matched this site** — the message is a template literal with a variable interpolation (`` `Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"` ``), not a static-literal `serverError("…")` form. The scanner at `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts:145` matches `/^(\s*)return\s+serverError\(\s*"([^"]+)"\s*\);?\s*$/` — the double-quoted string regex. Template literals with backticks fall through, so the scanner intentionally doesn't flag them. The dynamic-message rule (session 98 P-4) excluded them by design.

**Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` is literally `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `serverError(message)` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:75-83`). The migrated site's log line and 500 response body are identical to the inline form for all `Error`-instance inputs (the realistic case for a `try` block around `fs.readFileSync` / `JSON.parse` / `statSync`).

**Variable session ID preserved in the message** — the 4th-arg to `serverErrorFromCatch` is the *user-facing* error string. Even though the static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` scanner doesn't match template-literal-with-interpolation, the message itself is correctly preserved as a string template (the variable session ID stays in the error response — only the *composition* is collapsed). A user who hits a 500 from this endpoint still sees the specific session ID they requested, which is what the original inline form produced.

### 2. Source-pattern test comment update

Updated the stale comment in `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts:26-37` that previously said:

> The `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:255-256` site uses a dynamic template literal message and is exempt per the dynamic-message rule.

→ now reads:

> The `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` originally had a 2-line `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to read session "${sanitizedId}"\`)` pair at line 255-256; the message is a template-literal-with-variable-interpolation, NOT a static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` form, so the scanner never matched it. The site was migrated to `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` in session 129 (the variable session ID stays in the message — only the composition is collapsed). The dynamic-message rule (session 98 P-4) still excludes template-literal messages from this scanner, so the migration is belt-and-suspenders rather than a scanner-enforced invariant.

The update keeps the comment in sync with the post-migration code. The scanner logic itself is unchanged.

- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0` clean
- `npm run build` passes
- **Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` is textually identical to `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `return serverError(message)` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:75-83`). The migration is a name change, not a behavior change.

### Files

- `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts` (MODIFIED) — comment update only (scanner logic unchanged)

Net diff: **1 source file + 1 test comment update, +6 / -3 lines** (the migration = 2 import changes + 5-line helper call replacing 2-line inline block).

### Why only 1 site?

The List 1 surface (api/sessions, api/memory, api/logs, api/monitor) was already exhaustively audited for `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sites in session 125 (5 sites migrated). The session-129 pre-audit (the `rg --multiline` pass that found the dynamic-message site at `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts:255-256`) confirmed zero remaining static-literal sites in the List 1 surface, and the only **dynamic-message** site in List 1 territory is the one migrated in this session. The other dynamic-message sites in the codebase are in List 2 (`api/cron/hardware/route.ts`, all 4 migrated in the session-128 carryover) and List 4 (`api/mission-categories/route.ts`, `api/credentials/route.ts`, `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts`, `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts`, `api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts` — all out of scope for a List 1 pick).

### What was NOT migrated (and why)

- **`api/memory/hindsight/route.ts` dynamic-message sites** — `handleHealth`, `handleCount`, and the GET main catch all have `error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "…"` patterns, not `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(e).message}\`)` patterns. They use the `messageFromError` migration (closed in session 128) rather than the `serverErrorFromError` migration (the carryover helper from session 128 covers the latter shape). The two migrations target different inline forms; the Hindsight route's 3 sites are already done.
- **`api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22`** — has `return serverError(toError(e).message)` (bare message propagation, no static prefix). Out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` (no prefix → no `prefix + caught error's message` template-literal form). Already documented in the carryover test's "What the scanner does NOT match" section.
- **`api/monitor/route.ts:151-158`** — the inline `NextResponse.json({data}, {headers})` form is used because the route sets a `Cache-Control` header on the response. `ok()` is hard-locked to status 200 with no headers option. The migration would require a `okWithHeaders` factory (an API surface change), which is out of scope for this session.
- **`api/sessions/[id]/route.ts` 2 inner-catch `logApiError` calls (lines 127, 221)** — these are non-returning catch blocks (Hermes state.db read failure → fall through to file-based lookup; JSONL line parse failure → return `{index, raw}`). They correctly use the bare `logApiError` form because there's no `return serverError(...)` to compose with.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session.** List 1 has now been hit 2 sessions in a row (128, 129). **List 2 is the next-ripe surface** — last touched in the session-128 carryover (the `serverErrorFromError` 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts`). The cross-list follow-ups from the carryover: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82` (both have bare `return serverError(toError(e).message)` forms that are out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` but could benefit from a bare-message helper if a 2nd consumer appears). **List 3** also has follow-ups: the `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sites in `api/credentials/route.ts` and `api/mission-categories/route.ts` (3 sites, already audited).
2. **Promote a `bareServerError(e)` helper** when a 2nd consumer of the bare `return serverError(toError(e).message)` form appears. Currently 2 sites: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82`. The Rule of Three says: don't add the helper until a 3rd site appears.
3. **Migrate the 4 inline `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "…"` sites in `src/lib/`, `src/components/cron/`, and `src/app/recroom/`** to a server-side `toMessage(e, fallback)` or extend `messageFromError` to accept a generic consumer. The 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough to justify the helper. Multi-session sweep.
4. **`useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx` and `(main)/logs/page.tsx` both have URL-builder closures that could be lifted into the hook. Defer until the hook shape is stable across both pages.
5. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1200+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
6. **HindsightBrowser `error ?? "…"` followup** — 4 inline `error ?? "…"` sites that would benefit from a `runMutation`-shaped variant. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same shape.

## Session 128 cron carryover — `serverErrorFromError` helper + 4-site migration in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts`

The session 128 cron job left uncommitted work that this session finalised. The work was List 2 territory (the `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` is a Cron page endpoint, not a List 1 page) but the helper itself is generic — promoting it in a cron session and finishing it in a List 1 session is a slight scope-drift, justified by the helper's home-module consistency (both `serverErrorFromCatch` and `serverErrorFromError` live in `@/lib/api-logger`).

### What the carryover contains

**1 new helper** — `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` in `src/lib/api-logger.ts:140-148`. Sister-helper of `serverErrorFromCatch` for the dynamic-message-with-prefix variant. The composition is `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `serverError(\`${prefix}: ${toError(error).message}\`)`.

**1 migration** — `api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (4 sites: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE handlers, all originally the 2-line `logApiError + return serverError(\`Failed to X hardware cron: ${toError(e).message}\`)` pattern). All 4 sites collapsed to `serverErrorFromError(ROUTE, CONTEXT, e, "Failed to X hardware cron")`.

**2 new test files** (19 tests total):
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error.test.ts` (256 lines, 8 tests) — byte-equivalence matrix comparing the helper's output to the inline form for 6 different caught-value shapes (Error, empty Error, string, null, undefined, TypeError). Same shape as the existing `server-error-from-catch.test.ts`.
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts` (313 lines, 11 tests) — source-pattern audit test that scans the List 2 surface (api/cron/hardware) for un-migrated `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(...).message}\`)` sites. Per-list (matches the per-list-pick budget) and uses the same balanced-brace walker the session-112 carryover added.

### Why a sister-helper (not an extension of `serverErrorFromCatch`)

The two helpers have different message-source semantics:

- `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` — message is a **static literal** (e.g. `"Failed to list models"`). The caller supplies the full string.
- `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` — message is a **dynamic template-literal-with-prefix** (e.g. `` `Failed to read crontab: ${toError(e).message}` ``). The caller supplies a PREFIX, the helper concatenates the caught error's message.

A `prefix?` optional 4th-arg on `serverErrorFromCatch` would muddy the static-only contract that the source-pattern test relies on (the test's scanner at line 145 matches `serverError("STATIC")` only — adding a prefix variant would require a second scanner or a parameter-aware scanner). Sister-helpers with distinct signatures is clearer than an overloaded 4th-arg, and the byte-equivalence test in `server-error-from-error.test.ts:119-256` proves the helper matches the inline form for every reachable input shape.

### Files

- `src/lib/api-logger.ts` (MODIFIED) — added `serverErrorFromError` (8-line implementation + 60-line JSDoc) + `toError` added to import
- `src/app/api/cron/hardware/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — 1 import change + 4 site migrations
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error.test.ts` (NEW) — 8 tests, 256 lines
- `tests/unit/server-error-from-error-source-patterns.test.ts` (NEW) — 11 tests, 313 lines

Net diff: **1 source file + 1 test file (modified) + 2 test files (new) = 4 files**.

### Verification

- All 1653 unit tests pass (235 suites, +19 from the 2 new test files; the 4 migrated sites have no specific per-site test — the helper is exhaustively covered by `server-error-from-error.test.ts`, and the source-pattern test pins zero-tolerance for the dynamic-message-with-prefix form in the List 2 surface)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0` clean
- `npm run build` passes
- **Byte-equivalence** — `serverErrorFromError(route, context, error, prefix)` is textually identical to `logApiError(route, context, error)` + `` return serverError(`${prefix}: ${toError(error).message}`) `` (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:140-148`). The 8 unit tests in `server-error-from-error.test.ts` pin the byte-equivalence for 6 different caught-value shapes.

### What the scanner does NOT match (and why)

The source-pattern test's scanner at line 131 only matches the **canonical 2-line** `logApiError + return serverError(\`prefix: ${toError(e).message}\`)` shape. The "What the scanner does NOT match" section in the test header documents the gaps:
- `return serverError(toError(e).message)` — bare message propagation, no static prefix. Currently 2 sites: `api/cron/hardware/meta/route.ts:22` and `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts:82`. Out of scope for `serverErrorFromError` (no prefix → no template-literal form).
- `return serverError(\`Failed: ${var}\`)` without `toError` — different shape, not a `serverErrorFromError` candidate.
- `return serverError(msg)` where `msg` is a hoisted local variable — different shape, belongs in an EXEMPTIONS block.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Sister-helper with distinct signature" pattern** — when two related helpers have different message-source semantics (static literal vs dynamic template), prefer sister-helpers with distinct 4th-arg types (`message: string` vs `prefix: string`) over an overloaded optional 4th-arg. The clarity win is in the call site: `serverErrorFromCatch(...msg)` vs `serverErrorFromError(...prefix)` is self-documenting.
2. **"Per-list source-pattern test" pattern** — the test scope is `api/cron/hardware` only (List 2's surface for the dynamic-message-with-prefix form). Matches the per-list-pick budget from the rolling-PR protocol. Other lists can add their own per-list tests when the dynamic-message form appears in their surface.
3. **"Carryover finalisation in next session" pattern** — the previous cron job left uncommitted work that this session finalised. Per the rolling-PR protocol's carryover section, the verification (full suite + tsc + eslint + build) was re-run in this session, then the work was committed alongside the new List 1 work in a single commit chain.

## Session 128 — List 1 (Dashboard, Sessions, Memory, Logs) — `messageFromError` migration in `/api/memory/hindsight` + HindsightBrowser form-reset consolidation

Random pick: **List 1** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 1. The List 1 surface was closed for `serverErrorFromCatch` in session 125, but the broader `messageFromError()` migration (the cross-list "replace `instanceof Error ? err.message : \"…\"` with the canonical helper") had 3 leftover sites in `/api/memory/hindsight/route.ts` and a form-reset duplication in `HindsightBrowser.tsx`. This session closed both.

### 1. `messageFromError` migration in `/api/memory/hindsight/route.ts` (3 sites)

The route had three pre-`messageFromError` boilerplate sites that the canonical helper replaces:

- `handleHealth` catch (line 234): `error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Connection refused"` → `messageFromError(e, "Connection refused")`
- `handleCount` catch (line 249): `error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Unknown error"` → `messageFromError(e, "Unknown error")`
- GET main catch (line 309): `error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : "Hindsight error"` → `messageFromError(error, "Hindsight error")`

The neighbouring `hindsight-route-helpers.ts` already imports `messageFromError` (line 19), so the route file gets a 1-line import addition. All 3 sites are byte-equivalent for the realistic case (fetch rejections are always `Error` instances).

**Subtle behaviour change for the empty-Error trap** — `new Error("")` previously returned `""` from the inline form; the helper correctly returns the fallback (`"Hindsight error"`, etc.) via `toError().message || fallback`. This is the canonical "empty-Error trap" rule the rest of the codebase has standardised on (see `api-logger.ts` migration + `refactor-sweep-rolling-doc-safety` pitfall 8 for the verification matrix). The 3 sites are in error envelopes shown to the user, so the fallback behaviour is strictly better than an empty string.

**Documented divergence for non-Error throws** — JS allows `throw "string"`, `throw null`, etc. The helper coerces non-Error values via `String(value)` (so `throw 42` becomes `"42"`), while the inline form fell through to the fallback. The 3 sites all catch fetch rejections (always `Error` instances), so this divergence is unobservable in production. The new test pins both branches explicitly so a future reader doesn't try to "tighten" the helper back to the inline form.

### 2. HindsightBrowser form-reset consolidation (2 sites)

`HindsightBrowser.tsx` had two inline form-reset sites that the already-defined `closeDirectiveModal` / `closeModelModal` callbacks collapse:

- `handleCreateDirective.onSuccess` (lines 225-226): inline `setShowDirectiveModal(false); setDirForm(EMPTY_DIR_FORM);` → `closeDirectiveModal()`
- `handleCreateModel.onSuccess` (lines 316-317): inline `setShowModelModal(false); setModelForm(EMPTY_MODEL_FORM);` → `closeModelModal()`

The `closeDirectiveModal` and `closeModelModal` callbacks were already defined (lines 379-392) and used by the modal's X/overlay close paths. Promoting the save-success path to use them means the reset is in exactly one place per modal — a future "I added a `description` field to the directive modal" lands in one place (the `EMPTY_DIR_FORM` constant at the top), not in 4 scattered reset sites.

The save-success path is invoked AFTER all the component body's `useCallback` declarations have completed, so the forward reference is safe (JS closures capture by binding, not by value, and the `const` binding is fully resolved by the time the user clicks "Save").

### 3. Test coverage — `tests/unit/hindsight-message-from-error.test.ts` (new, 9 tests)

Locks in the `messageFromError` byte-equivalence for the 3 migrated sites and pins the empty-Error trap + non-Error-throw behaviour so the divergence from the inline form is documented, not accidental.

Test groups:
- GET catch site (fallback = "Hindsight error") — populated Error, empty-Error trap
- handleHealth site (fallback = "Connection refused") — populated Error, empty-Error trap
- handleCount site (fallback = "Unknown error") — populated Error, empty-Error trap
- Documented non-Error-throw behaviour (unreachable in production) — string, number, undefined/null

All 9 new tests pass. Combined with the existing 4 HindsightBrowser render tests (which exercise the close-callback paths), the refactor is fully covered.

## Session 127 — List 3 (Models, Agents, Skills, Tools, Personalities) — `serverErrorFromCatch` 6-site List 3 migration + List 3 source-pattern surface assertion

Random pick: **List 3** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 3, the first List 3 pick since session 119. The prior session (126) explicitly listed the cross-list `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sweep as the next-ripe work — Lists 1, 2, and 4 are all zero-tolerance, but **List 3 still had 6 static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` sites** in `api/{skills/*, tools}/`. This session closed the gap.

### Why List 3 was the only remaining surface

Session 125 closed the 5 List 1 sites in `api/{sessions, sessions/[id], logs, monitor}/`. Session 95 closed the 27 List 4 sites in `api/{config, config/[section], config/seed, agent/files/[key], agent/profiles, fs/git/branches, models/*, ...}`. Session 126 confirmed the List 2 surface is empty (every `serverError(...)` site in `api/{cron, missions, mission-categories, orchestration/chat, ...}` is either dynamic-message, variable-msg, or interleaved with side effects). That left **List 3** — and the audit found exactly 6 static-literal sites in `api/skills/*` + `api/tools/route.ts`.

### Refactor — 6 static-literal `serverErrorFromCatch` migrations

| File | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| `src/app/api/skills/route.ts` | 100 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to list skills")` → `return serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/skills", "listing skills", error, "Failed to list skills")` |
| `src/app/api/skills/[name]/route.ts` | 58 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to read skill")` → `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` |
| `src/app/api/skills/[name]/route.ts` | 119 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to write skill")` → `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` |
| `src/app/api/skills/[name]/toggle/route.ts` | 89 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to toggle skill")` → `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` |
| `src/app/api/skills/[...path]/route.ts` | 81 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to read skill")` → `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` |
| `src/app/api/tools/route.ts` | 26 | `logApiError + return serverError("Failed to load toolset catalog")` → `serverErrorFromCatch(...)` |

**Why this matters:** every site was the canonical 2-line `logApiError(...)` + `return serverError(STATIC)` pattern — the byte-level definition of a `serverErrorFromCatch` candidate. Each migration collapses 4 lines (the two call lines + their opening/closing parens) into a single 6-arg call. The helper centralizes the "log + 500 + canonical `{ error: <message> }` body" contract that was duplicated across 75+ catch blocks. The 6 sites also benefit from cleaner imports (drop `logApiError` and `serverError` from the 4 files where the symbols are no longer used; `[...path]/route.ts` still uses `logApiError` for the inner subdir-loop try/catch on line 63, so the import stays).

**Byte-equivalence:** `serverErrorFromCatch(route, context, error, message)` is the canonical implementation of the inline form per `src/lib/api-logger.ts` (lines 90-99). Same `logApiError` call (same `[API <route>] Error <context>: <error.message>` console line), same `serverError(message)` return (same 500 status + same `{ error: <message> }` body). The 4 `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch.test.ts` contract tests (session 95 carryover) cover the log+response pair end-to-end.

### Source-pattern test extension — List 3 surface added

`tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts` was extended with a new assertion: `has zero \`logApiError + return serverError(STATIC)\` sites in the List 3 surface (api/skills, api/tools)`. The test reuses the same scanner as the List 1 assertion (balanced-paren walker for `logApiError(...)` + single-line regex for `return serverError(STATIC_LITERAL);`) and filters sites by file path prefix (`src/app/api/skills` or `src/app/api/tools`). The describe-block was renamed from "(List 1 surface)" to "(List 1 + List 3 surfaces)" and the file-top history comment was extended with a "Session 127 history" block.

**Why this matters:** the List 3 zero-tolerance rule now keeps the surface clean as future PRs touch `api/skills/*` and `api/tools/*`. Any new static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` site added to those files will fail the test until migrated to `serverErrorFromCatch`. The scanner's regex intentionally does NOT match dynamic-message sites (template literals, string concatenation, `error.message` propagation), so the 4 dynamic-message sites elsewhere in List 3 are correctly exempt.

### What was rejected (and why)

- **Migrating `api/skills/[name]/route.ts:104` `serverError(push.error ?? "Push failed")` to `serverErrorFromCatch`.** The `?? "Push failed"` fallback makes the message dynamic, not static. The scanner's regex requires a pure string literal, so this site is never a candidate. Migrating would require restructuring the `if (!push.success)` branch to use a static `serverErrorFromCatch` call (e.g. always return `serverErrorFromCatch("PUT /api/skills/[name]", "writing skill ${name}", new Error(push.error), "Push failed")`) — that's a control-flow change that propagates `push.error` to the catch path, which is a behavior change. Out of scope.
- **Migrating `api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts:45` `serverError(toError(error).message)` to `serverErrorFromCatch`.** Dynamic-message (`toError(error).message` propagation). Session 124 P-3 EXEMPT. The `api/models/*` GET/POST/PUT/DELETE routes already use `serverErrorFromCatch` (adopted during the List 4 sweep).
- **Migrating `api/agent/files/[key]/route.ts`.** Already on `serverErrorFromCatch` from an earlier session (the `buildFileResponse` helper extracted in session 52 also touched this surface).
- **Auditing `api/agent/profiles/*` for new static-literal sites.** Already on `serverErrorFromCatch` (adopted during the List 4 sweep + session 119's `applyProfileOrRootPatch` delegation).
- **Cross-list `serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` sweep extension to `api/orchestration/chat`, `api/memory/*`, `api/agent/*`.** No static-literal sites in any of these surfaces (all dynamic-message, variable-msg, or already-migrated). The scanner would find zero sites. The 4 dynamic-message sites in `api/memory/*` and `api/orchestration/chat/*` are correctly exempt.
- **Adding a `setErrorFromCaught`-shaped variant for useState error reporting in the api/skills/* routes.** The api/skills/* routes are server-side, not client-side. The client-side equivalent (`useApiData` + `setErrorFromCaught`) was already adopted in session 126.
- **Extracting the 4 subdir-loop `logApiError` calls in `api/skills/[...path]/route.ts:63` to a `logSubdirError` helper.** The 4 calls (one per subdir — `references`, `templates`, `scripts`, `assets`) have a 1-arg `subdirPath` concatenation that varies per call. The pattern is "best-effort read of optional subdirs" — a single helper would need a `subdir: string, err: unknown` shape that's 2 args, which is the same complexity as the inline form. Defer.

### Files

## Session 126 — List 2 (Cron, Missions, Chat) — `logCronSyncFailure` helper + 2 site migration + `useApiData` `setErrorFromCaught`

Random pick: **List 2** via `echo $((RANDOM % 4 + 1))` = 2, the first List 2 pick since session 118. The prior session (125) explicitly recommended List 2 as the next-ripe surface for the `serverErrorFromCatch` cross-list follow-up. This session re-audited the List 2 surface against the session 124 P-3 discriminator (static-literal vs dynamic-message vs variable-msg) and found that **the static-literal `serverErrorFromCatch` migration surface in List 2 is empty** — every `logApiError + return serverError(...)` site in `api/{cron/hardware, mission-categories, orchestration/chat, cron/hardware/meta}/` is either dynamic-message (template literal / `toError(e).message`) or variable-msg (hoisted `const msg` for substring branching). The only truly static-literal `serverError("Failed to create cron job for mission")` site in `api/missions/route.ts:352` is interleaved with `updateMission` + `appendAuditLine` side effects between the `logApiError` and the `return`, which makes it a scanner non-match (and not a clean `serverErrorFromCatch` candidate without restructuring).

So the session pivoted to two high-leverage List 2 cleanups that the prior "mined clean" verdicts didn't catch:

1. **New `logCronSyncFailure` helper** in `src/lib/cron-sync-failure.ts` to replace the side-effect-only misuse of `cronSyncFailureResponse` at 2 sites (`api/missions/route.ts:329` and `lib/mission-promote-handler.ts:177`). Both callers were calling `cronSyncFailureResponse(route, pushResult)` purely for its `logApiError` side effect and discarding the returned `NextResponse`, then rebuilding the same body inline to splice extra fields (`data: { mission: ... }` for the missions site, `mission: ...` for the promote site). The misuse was misleading — a reader scanning the call site would expect the response to be returned. The new `logCronSyncFailure(route, pushResult)` helper is the side-effect-only companion, pairing cleanly with `cronSyncFailureBody` for the response payload. 4 new unit tests pin the contract (returns undefined, identical console line to `cronSyncFailureResponse`, "unknown" fallback, verbatim push error).
2. **`useApiData` setError migration** — the only `setError(messageFromError(...))` site left in production code is `src/hooks/useApiData.ts:145`. This is the shared `useApiData` hook used by `(main)/logs/page.tsx`, `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`, `(main)/sessions/[id]/page.tsx`, `(main)/logs/page.tsx`, and several others (the `api/data` consumers across all 4 lists). Migrating to `setErrorFromCaught(setError, e, "Request failed")` is byte-equivalent per session 91 P-2 (the verbose `toError(err).message || fallback` form is the same as `messageFromError(err, fallback)`) and removes one intermediate variable. Single high-leverage cleanup that touches every page using `useApiData`.

### Refactor 1 — `logCronSyncFailure` helper + 2-site migration

| File | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/cron-sync-failure.ts` | new export | `logCronSyncFailure(route, pushResult): void` — same `logApiError` call as `cronSyncFailureResponse`, no `NextResponse` produced |
| `src/app/api/missions/route.ts` | 329 | `cronSyncFailureResponse("POST /api/missions", pushResult);` (discarded return) → `logCronSyncFailure("POST /api/missions", pushResult);` |
| `src/lib/mission-promote-handler.ts` | 177 | `cronSyncFailureResponse("promoteMission", pushResult);` (discarded return) → `logCronSyncFailure("promoteMission", pushResult);` |
| `tests/unit/cron-sync-failure.test.ts` | new `describe("logCronSyncFailure")` block | 4 unit tests pinning the side-effect contract |

**Why this matters:** the side-effect-only misuse was misleading. `cronSyncFailureResponse` is named after its return value, and a reader scanning `cronSyncFailureResponse("POST /api/missions", pushResult);` (no `return`!) would naturally expect to find the response discarded for some reason — but the comment had to explain that the call was for its side effect. With the new helper, the call site reads `logCronSyncFailure("POST /api/missions", pushResult);` — the name documents intent, no comment is needed. The 2 migrated sites also get cleaner imports (drop `cronSyncFailureResponse`, add `logCronSyncFailure`).

**Byte-equivalence:** `logCronSyncFailure` and the `logApiError` call inside `cronSyncFailureResponse` produce the EXACT same `console.error` line for the same `(route, pushResult)` inputs. The "logs the same line as `cronSyncFailureResponse` for the same inputs" test compares the two console-error strings directly. The response body is unchanged at both call sites (the inline `NextResponse.json` and the inline `return { ... }` objects still call `cronSyncFailureBody(pushResult)` to build the canonical body — the migration only swaps the logging helper, not the body).

### Refactor 2 — `useApiData` `setErrorFromCaught` migration

| File | Line | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| `src/hooks/useApiData.ts` | 145 | `setError(messageFromError(e, "Request failed"))` → `setErrorFromCaught(setError, e, "Request failed")` |

**Why this matters:** `useApiData` is the shared hook used by `(main)/logs/page.tsx`, `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`, `(main)/sessions/[id]/page.tsx`, `config/page.tsx`, and several others. After session 91's `setErrorFromCaught` helper landed, this was the LAST `setError(messageFromError(...))` site in production code (the 2 other hits in `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:203,223` are JSDoc examples for the helper definition itself). With this migration, the verbose `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` form is **fully eliminated from the codebase** — the helper is now the universal convention for catch-block `useState<string \| null>` error reporting.

**Byte-equivalence:** `setErrorFromCaught(setError, e, fallback)` is the canonical implementation of `setError(messageFromError(e, fallback))` (`src/lib/api-fetch.ts:218-224`). The migration is a name change, not a behavior change. The verbose `setError(toError(err).message || "...")` form is byte-equivalent to the same `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` form because `toError(err).message || fallback` is literally the body of `messageFromError` (per session 91 P-2). The new code preserves the empty-Error trap: `setErrorFromCaught` calls `messageFromError(e, fallback)` which returns `""` for `new Error("")` (preserving the inline form's empty-Error behavior).

### What was rejected (and why)

- **Migrating the 7 inline `serverError(toError(error).message || "...")` sites in `api/{mission-categories, orchestration/chat, cron/hardware/meta}/` to `serverErrorFromCatch`.** All 7 are dynamic-message sites per the session 124 P-3 discriminator (`toError(err).message` propagation OR `|| "static fallback"` propagation). Migrating would change the response body from the error's actual message to the static fallback string, which is a behavior change. The 4 sites with `||` fallbacks have inline comments explicitly explaining the byte-equivalence is preserved (e.g. `mission-categories/route.ts:59-60`). Exempt by definition.
- **Migrating the 4 template-literal `serverError(\`Failed to X: ${toError(e).message}\`)` sites in `api/cron/hardware/route.ts:248,355,448,505`.** Dynamic with static prefix. Session 124 P-3 EXEMPT.
- **Migrating the 3 hoisted-msg sites in `api/mission-categories/route.ts:91,172` (and the matching `serverError(msg)` at 96, 177).** The `msg` is computed once and used for substring branching (e.g. `if (msg.includes("already exists"))` for the 409 site). The session 123 P-3 "err + variable-status" exemption case — out of scanner scope, belongs in an EXEMPTIONS doc block.
- **Migrating `api/missions/route.ts:352` `serverError("Failed to create cron job for mission")` to `serverErrorFromCatch`.** The site is the only true static-literal `serverError(STATIC)` site in List 2, but it has `updateMission` + `appendAuditLine` interleaved between the `logApiError` and the `return serverError`. `serverErrorFromCatch` does `logApiError + return serverError(STATIC)` as a single call — the interleaved side effects block a clean migration. Restructuring to move the side effects before/after would be a control-flow change. Skipped.
- **Refactoring `useApiData` to a 3-state error union (idle | error).** The current `error: string | null` contract is byte-equivalent and used by every consumer. A state-union refactor is a signature change with type-level ripples. Out of scope.
- **Extracting `useApiData`'s `mountedRef` + `optionsRef` + `useEffect` polling loop into a `useApiDataLifecycle` hook.** Cosmetic split, doesn't reduce code. Defer.
- **Auditing the 6 `err instanceof Error` sites in `src/lib/` and `src/components/cron/` for a `toMessage(e, fallback)` helper** (the 7 `src/lib/hermes-profile-sync.ts` sites alone are enough per session 90's "next session should" #2). Multi-session sweep; defer.
- **Adding a `setErrorFromCaught`-shaped variant that accepts a `prevState: T | null` updater.** None of the current consumers use the updater form. Rule of Three says don't add the parameter until the 2nd consumer appears.
- **Replacing `apiFetch<{ data?: T }>(...) + envelope.data ?? null` in `useApiData:121-131` with the `safeApiCallData<T>(url, init) → T \| null` helper from session 123 P-1.** The hook already does the right thing — it handles `T | undefined → T | null` coercion via `?? null` and documents the contract. The `safeApiCallData` helper would work but is for read-only `{ data: T }` envelope fetches without `.ok` / `.error` consumption. The hook could use it, but the current `apiFetch` form is more explicit about the envelope shape. Cosmetic preference, not a clear win. Defer.
- **Consolidating the 2 `logApiError + return serverError(STATIC_LITERAL)` migrations into a `serverErrorFromCatch` extension with optional side-effect callback.** The `updateMission` + `appendAuditLine` interleaving in `api/missions/route.ts:348-353` doesn't fit a clean callback API. A callback-based helper would need a `{ pre?: (err) => void, post?: (err) => void }` shape that's more complex than the inline catch block. Out of scope.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Side-effect-only helper extraction" pattern** — when a helper that returns a value is misused purely for a side effect, split the side effect into a separate named helper. The new helper's name documents the intent (`logCronSyncFailure` reads as "log a sync failure"; the old `cronSyncFailureResponse` misread as "respond with a sync failure"). The 2 migrated sites also benefit from cleaner imports (drop the unused `cronSyncFailureResponse` import, add the new `logCronSyncFailure` import).
2. **"The last `setError(messageFromError(...))` site in production code"** — after session 91's `setErrorFromCaught` helper landed, the only remaining verbose site was the shared `useApiData` hook. Migrating it closes the loop: every `setError(messageFromError(err, "..."))` call site in production code now uses the helper. The 2 remaining `src/lib/api-fetch.ts:203,223` hits are JSDoc examples for the helper definition itself. The audit recipe `rg -nU 'setError\(messageFromError' src/ | grep -v 'api-fetch\|tests/'` returns 0 hits post-session.
3. **"List 2 serverErrorFromCatch surface is empty"** — every `logApiError + return serverError(...)` site in List 2 (api/cron, api/cron/hardware, api/cron/hardware/meta, api/missions, api/mission-categories, api/orchestration/chat) is either dynamic-message, variable-msg, or interleaved with side effects. The session 124 P-3 discriminator applies to all 11 List 2 sites. Future sessions that pick List 2 for `serverErrorFromCatch` will find the same empty surface — pick a different refactor target.

### Refactor — 5 static-literal `logApiError + serverError` sites → `serverErrorFromCatch`

Each site collapses from:

```typescript
} catch (error) {
  logApiError("METHOD /api/<route>", "<context>", error);
  return serverError("Failed to ...");
}
```

to:

```typescript
} catch (error) {
  return serverErrorFromCatch("METHOD /api/<route>", "<context>", error, "Failed to ...");
}
```

| # | File | Site | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/app/api/sessions/route.ts` | GET handler :71-72 | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/sessions", "listing sessions", error, "Failed to load sessions")`. `logApiError` import dropped (no other consumer in this file); `serverError` removed from `@/lib/api-response` import. |
| 2 | `src/app/api/sessions/route.ts` | POST handler :140-141 | `serverErrorFromCatch("POST /api/sessions", "session action", error, "Failed to process session action")`. Same import drops as #1. |
| 3 | `src/app/api/logs/route.ts` | GET handler :98-99 | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/logs", "reading logs", error, "Failed to read logs")`. `serverError` removed from `@/lib/api-response` import. `logApiError` import preserved (still used at line 65 for the best-effort `availableLogs` list). |
| 4 | `src/app/api/logs/route.ts` | DELETE handler :139-140 | `serverErrorFromCatch("DELETE /api/logs", "deleting log", error, "Failed to delete logs")`. Same import changes as #3. |
| 5 | `src/app/api/monitor/route.ts` | GET handler :161-162 | `serverErrorFromCatch("GET /api/monitor", "aggregating monitor data", error, "Failed to read system monitor data")`. `logApiError` import dropped (no other consumer); `serverError` import dropped (only consumer was the migrated site). |

### New audit guard — `server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts`

Added `tests/unit/server-error-from-catch-source-patterns.test.ts` (303 lines, 7 tests) — the sister to `ok-factory-source-patterns.test.ts`. The new test:

1. **Scans the api/ tree** with a balanced-paren walker for `logApiError(...)` calls followed (within 2 lines) by a `return serverError(STATIC_LITERAL);` pattern. The walker correctly handles multi-line `logApiError` calls and the single-line static-literal `serverError` shape.
2. **Asserts zero matches in the List 1 surface** (`api/sessions`, `api/memory`, `api/logs`, `api/monitor`). Pinned for the post-session-125 zero-tolerance rule — any future static-literal site in the List 1 surface will fail this test.
3. **Documents EXEMPTIONS** for the wider codebase's dynamic-message sites (10 entries across `api/sessions/[id]/route.ts`, `api/mission-categories/route.ts`, `api/orchestration/chat/route.ts`, `api/models/fallbacks/sync/route.ts`, `api/cron/hardware/route.ts`) — none of these are in the List 1 surface, but they're documented in the source file as the cross-list canonical exemption for the dynamic-message family.
4. **Includes 4 scanner form-coverage self-tests:**
   - Form 1: catches the canonical 2-line catch-block (✓)
   - Negative: `logApiError` followed by `badRequest` (NOT caught — different shape)
   - Negative: bare `return serverError(...)` without `logApiError` (NOT caught — out of scope)
   - Negative: dynamic-message `serverError(\`template\`)` (NOT caught — exempt per dynamic-message rule)

The test's scanner is intentionally narrower than the `ok()` scanner: it only matches the canonical 2-line static-literal shape, because that's exactly what `serverErrorFromCatch` is byte-equivalent to. The scanner's contract is pinned by the 4 self-tests so future scanner changes that break coverage fail immediately.

### Refactor 1 detail — `fs/git/branches/route.ts`

The catch block was the canonical `logApiError + return NextResponse.json({ error, status: 500 })` pair. `serverErrorFromCatch` is the byte-equivalent shim (per `src/lib/api-logger.ts:75`): it composes the two primitives (`logApiError` + `serverError`) and returns the same 500 + `{ error: <message> }` response.

```typescript
// before
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { logApiError } from "@/lib/api-logger";
// ...
  } catch (error) {
    logApiError("GET /api/fs/git/branches", "git info", error);
    return NextResponse.json({ error: "Failed to read git branches" }, { status: 500 });
  }

// after
import { NextRequest } from "next/server";
import { serverErrorFromCatch } from "@/lib/api-logger";
// ...
  } catch (error) {
    return serverErrorFromCatch(
      "GET /api/fs/git/branches",
      "git info",
      error,
      "Failed to read git branches",
    );
  }
```

**Byte-equivalence audit:**

- `serverErrorFromCatch` is literally `logApiError(route, context, error); return serverError(message);` (`src/lib/api-logger.ts:75-83`). Same log line: `[API GET /api/fs/git/branches] Error git info: <error.message>`. Same response: 500 + `{ error: "Failed to read git branches" }`.
- The 4 args (route + context + error + message) map 1:1 to the inline form's 2 statements. No semantic drift.
- The `error` variable flows through `logApiError` → `messageFromError(err, "")` (the session 98 byte-equivalence fallback) → console.error. The pre-refactor inline form called `logApiError` directly with the same `error` arg, which dispatched through the same `messageFromError` call. The log line is byte-identical.

### Test mock update — `fs-git-branches-api.test.ts`

Per session 121 P-1 + session 119 P-3, the canonical `jest.requireActual` template re-exports the real `serverErrorFromCatch` from the mocked `@/lib/api-logger` module:

```typescript
jest.mock("@/lib/api-logger", () => ({
  logApiError: jest.fn(),
  // serverErrorFromCatch is the canonical 2-line catch-block shim that
  // composes logApiError + serverError. The real helper from
  // @/lib/api-logger is used here so the test exercises the same
  // wire-format contract as production (status 500, body
  // { error: <message> }).
  serverErrorFromCatch: jest.requireActual("@/lib/api-logger").serverErrorFromCatch,
}));
```

This is the "preserve the real helper, mock only the side-effects" pattern. The test's existing `jest.fn()` for `logApiError` still works for assertion (the new test doesn't assert on `logApiError` calls but the mock is preserved for any future test that does). The real `serverErrorFromCatch` exercises the full wire-format contract end-to-end.

### New byte-equivalence test

```typescript
it("returns 500 with the static 'Failed to read git branches' message when readGitBranchMetadataForWorkspacePath throws (serverErrorFromCatch byte-equivalence)", async () => {
  mockReadGit.mockRejectedValue(new Error("git binary not found"));
  const { GET } = await import("@/app/api/fs/git/branches/route");
  const res = await GET(
    new NextRequest("http://localhost/api/fs/git/branches?path=/home/tester/repo"),
  );
  expect(res.status).toBe(500);
  const body = await res.json();
  expect(body).toEqual({ error: "Failed to read git branches" });
});
```

This locks the contract: the static error message "Failed to read git branches" is the user-facing response body, regardless of what the underlying `error.message` is. The `serverErrorFromCatch` helper uses the static 4th arg as the response body, NOT the `error.message` — this is the "Shape 1" pattern from `refactor-sweep-rolling-doc-safety` Pitfall 4.

## Session 123 — List 4 `ok()` factory migration + 4th list-surface test (carryover commit)

This session is a **carryover commit** for the work session 123 verified-green (tsc + eslint + 1612/1612 jest + npm run build) but hit the iteration cap before the final commit + push + PR-update cycle. Per the new **Mode F** carryover detection recipe (documented in `references/session-123-list4-ok-factory-sweep.md`), the resolution is the 4-step commit-when-verified protocol: (1) verify green, (2) commit, (3) push, (4) update PR body — THEN start new work.

**The session 123 work, re-summarised:**

The `ok()` factory (added in `src/lib/api-response.ts:197`) collapses `return NextResponse.json({ data: <expr> });` to `return ok(<expr>);`. The migration is byte-equivalent — `ok<T>(data)` is literally `NextResponse.json({ data }, { status: 200 })`. The factory had been swept across Lists 1, 2, and 3 in sessions 111-119 (per `ok-factory-source-patterns.test.ts` per-list assertions) but List 4 was never touched. This session closed the gap.

Walked the List 4 surface (`src/app/config/**`, `src/app/api/config/**`, `src/app/api/models/**`, `src/components/models/**`, `src/hooks/useModelsPage.ts`, `src/lib/config-schema.ts`, `src/lib/secret-mask.ts`, `src/lib/env-line.ts`, `src/lib/sync-manager.ts`). - **Refactor 1** (HIGH value): `src/app/api/config/route.ts` PUT handler had 3× `getActiveHermesPaths()` calls (lines 175, 190 + the inline `if existsSync(configPath) { const H = getActiveHermesPaths(); const backupDir = H.backups; ensureDir(backupDir); ... }` block at 175-182). The inline 4-line backup block re-implemented `backupFile()` from `@/lib/fs-helpers` (promoted from a private helper in session 85) — the canonical helper handles `existsSync + ensureDir + backupTimestamp + writeFileSync` in 4 lines and returns `null` for missing source. - **Refactor 2** (MEDIUM value): `src/app/config/page.tsx` `SectionCard` and `QuickLinkCard` had ~32 lines of identical `<Link><div><Icon /><ChevronRight /></div><h3>title</h3><p>description</p><div>footer</div></Link>` chrome, differing only in the footer badges. - **Refactor 3** (LOW value, but high consistency): `src/components/models/ModelSyncButtons.tsx:182` had a raw `fetch() + headers + !res.ok + .json()` block — the only such site in the entire models surface. `apiFetch<T>()` from `@/lib/api-fetch` is the canonical helper (used in 30+ List 1-4 sites). 7 lines → 3 lines + the LSP-shadowed `fetch` warning goes away.

### Refactor

| # | File | Lines | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/app/api/config/route.ts` | 175-190 (12 lines) → 172-187 (3 lines) | Hoist `const paths = getActiveHermesPaths()`; replace 4-line `if (existsSync(configPath)) { ... writeFileSync(backupPath, ...) }` block with `backupFile(paths.config, paths.backups)`; reuse `paths.config` for the write-back line. Imports changed `writeFileSync + existsSync` for backup block: dropped `ensureDir + backupTimestamp` (now in `backupFile`). |
| 2 | `src/app/config/page.tsx` | new 58-102 (CardLink) + 104-189 (SectionCard/QuickLinkCard slimmed) | Extract `CardLink` shell with `footer?: React.ReactNode` prop. `SectionCard` and `QuickLinkCard` each drop their `<Link>` chrome block (~32 lines each) and supply a footer fragment. |
| 3 | `src/components/models/ModelSyncButtons.tsx` | 182-188 (7 lines) → 183-189 (3 lines) | Replace raw `fetch() + headers + !res.ok + .json()` with `apiFetch<{ data?: { diffs?: DiffEntry[] } }>(url, { method, body: JSON.stringify(...) })`. Drop the local `as { data?: ... }` cast — `apiFetch<T>` returns the typed shape. |

### Refactor 1 detail — `config/route.ts` PUT handler

The 3 `getActiveHermesPaths()` calls were a code-smell — each call rebuilds the path bundle from env vars + active profile lookup. Hoisting to a single `const paths = getActiveHermesPaths()` cuts 2 redundant lookups per request and centralises the path resolution. The inline backup block re-implemented the canonical helper byte-for-byte (same `<basename>.<ts>.bak` naming, same UTF-8 read+write, same `existsSync` skip-on-missing).

```typescript
// before
try {
  const config = readCachedConfig();

  // Create backup
  const H = getActiveHermesPaths();
  const configPath = H.config;
  if (existsSync(configPath)) {
    const backupDir = H.backups;
    ensureDir(backupDir);
    const backupPath = `${backupDir}/config.yaml.${backupTimestamp()}.bak`;
    writeFileSync(backupPath, readFileSync(configPath, "utf-8"), "utf-8");
  }

  // Merge values into section
  const current = (config[section] as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
  config[section] = { ...current, ...(values as Record<string, unknown>) };

  // Write back
  const content = dumpYamlConfig(config);
  writeFileSync(getActiveHermesPaths().config, content, "utf-8");
  // ...

// after
try {
  const paths = getActiveHermesPaths();

  // Create backup (no-op when config.yaml doesn't exist) — single call
  // to the canonical backupFile() helper replaces the 4-line inline
  // `existsSync + ensureDir + backupTimestamp + writeFileSync` block.
  backupFile(paths.config, paths.backups);

  const config = readCachedConfig();

  // Merge values into section
  const current = (config[section] as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
  config[section] = { ...current, ...(values as Record<string, unknown>) };

  // Write back
  const content = dumpYamlConfig(config);
  writeFileSync(paths.config, content, "utf-8");
  // ...
```

**Byte-equivalence audit:**
- `getActiveHermesPaths()` is idempotent and cheap (env-var reads + 1 DB query for active profile in `getActiveHermesPaths`). 3 calls → 1 call is a perf + clarity win, not a behavior change.
- `backupFile(paths.config, paths.backups)` returns `null` when `config.yaml` doesn't exist (same as the inline `if existsSync(configPath) { ... }` skip). When the file exists, the helper produces the exact same `<basename>.<ts>.bak` filename in the same `paths.backups` dir with the same UTF-8 content. The 4 existing tests in `tests/unit/fs-helpers.test.ts` lock the helper's contract — they continue to pass unchanged.
- The pre-existing order in the original was: `readCachedConfig()` (line 172) → backup block (175-182) → merge (185) → write (190). The new order is: backup (177) → `readCachedConfig()` (179) → merge (182) → write (187). **The order swap is observable in one edge case**: if the backup block threw, the original would have already read+parsed the config and then crashed; the new order crashes before reading. This is a *strictly better* failure mode (we don't parse a file we're about to back up — we back up first, then parse), but technically a different code path on a backup-write failure. The behaviour on the success path is byte-identical.

**Why the order swap is safe in practice:**
- `backupFile()` only fails when `paths.backups` cannot be created (read-only fs) or the source file is unreadable (permission issue). Both cases would have failed the same way under the old code, just with a different `error.message` (a `writeFileSync` error vs a `backupFile` error). The `serverErrorFromCatch("PUT /api/config", "updating config", error, "Failed to update config")` wrapper catches both and returns the same 500 with the same `"Failed to update config"` body.
- The `readCachedConfig()` call's failure mode is unchanged — if `yaml.load(content)` throws, the original returned `{}` (line 49-52 of the original) and the new code does the same (the helper is called after the backup, but the helper calls happen in the same `try` block).
- The `Write` step on the new code (line 187) is the same `writeFileSync(paths.config, content, "utf-8")` as the old line 190.

**Why not use `atomicWriteFile()`:** the original code uses `writeFileSync` directly (not `atomicWriteFile`). The migration would be a behavior change on the *failure* path (atomic write + rollback vs plain write — the latter could leave a partial file on disk if the process is killed mid-write). Per the session-51 "AT LEAST identical results" rule, I left the write-back as `writeFileSync` and only changed the *backup* step. The `atomicWriteFile` migration is a separate concern that needs its own error-recovery test seam.

### Refactor 2 detail — `CardLink` shell

The 2 cards in `src/app/config/page.tsx` had identical Link chrome: the same `group rounded-xl border bg-dark-900/50 p-5 transition-all ${colorBorderMap[color]}` wrapper, the same `<div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-3">` header with the same `<ChevronRight />` hover-effect, the same `<h3>` title and `<p>` description, and the same footer container. The only difference was the footer content.

The new `CardLink` is a 25-line shell that takes `href + icon + title + description + color + footer?` and renders the same chrome. Each card now defines only its per-section content:

- `SectionCard` → footer is a 3-badge cluster (field count, "configured" indicator, complex-key count) for the section-detail view.
- `QuickLinkCard` → footer is a single badge for the cross-link callout (e.g. "dedicated editor" for Personalities).

**Byte-equivalence audit:**
- The new `CardLink` footer container is `<div className="mt-3 flex items-center gap-2 flex-wrap">`. The old `SectionCard` footer container was `<div className="mt-3 flex items-center gap-2">` (no `flex-wrap`). The new container adds `flex-wrap` — this is a *purely additive* style change (flex items that fit on one line still render in one line; only when the row overflows does the new property take effect). For all reachable inputs in `SectionCard` (1-3 badges per card), the layout is byte-equivalent. For the `QuickLinkCard` footer (a single inline-block span), the change from `<div className="mt-3">` to `<div className="mt-3 flex items-center gap-2 flex-wrap">` is byte-equivalent because a single flex child sits in the same left-aligned position with the same `gap-2` (no effect on a single child).
- All other chrome (Link, header div, h3, p, Icon, ChevronRight) is byte-for-byte identical to the pre-refactor components.

### Refactor 3 detail — `apiFetch` migration

The raw `fetch()` block in `ModelSyncButtons.tsx:182-188` was the only such site in the entire models surface. The codebase convention (set in session 56 and adopted by 30+ List 1-4 sites) is `apiFetch<T>()` from `@/lib/api-fetch`. The raw form was a pre-session-56 carryover that the session-77 sweep missed because `ModelSyncButtons.tsx` is a UI component (not a route handler) and the session-77 audit recipe was `grep -rn "fetch(" src/app/api/`.

```typescript
// before
const res = await fetch(`/api/models/${encodeURIComponent(modelId)}/diff`, {
  method: "POST",
  headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
  body: JSON.stringify({ direction }),
});
if (!res.ok) throw new Error("Failed to compute diffs");
const json = await res.json();
const diffs = (json as { data?: { diffs?: DiffEntry[] } })?.data?.diffs ?? [];

// after
const json = await apiFetch<{ data?: { diffs?: DiffEntry[] } }>(
  `/api/models/${encodeURIComponent(modelId)}/diff`,
  {
    method: "POST",
    body: JSON.stringify({ direction }),
  },
);
const diffs = json.data?.diffs ?? [];
```

**Byte-equivalence audit:**
- `apiFetch` sets `Content-Type: application/json` by default (it does `{ "Content-Type": "application/json", ...options?.headers }` so any caller-supplied header would override — there are none here, so the wire header is identical).
- `apiFetch` throws on non-2xx with the server's error message; the raw form threw `"Failed to compute diffs"` (a generic message). The error-message change is observable: a 500 from the diff route now throws the server's `error` field (e.g. `"Failed to compute diff"`) instead of the generic `"Failed to compute diffs"`. **However**, the call site is wrapped in a `try { ... } catch { /* fallback to generic messages */ }` (line 191-209), so the user-visible behaviour is unchanged: both errors are swallowed and the modal shows the same fallback diffs.
- The `(json as { data?: ... })` cast was needed because the raw `res.json()` returns `unknown` (well, `any`); `apiFetch<{ data?: { diffs?: DiffEntry[] } }>` is a typed cast on the call site, which TypeScript happily accepts. The runtime behaviour is identical (no actual type-check at runtime).

### Files

- `src/app/api/config/route.ts` (MODIFIED) — PUT handler: 3 calls → 1 call to `getActiveHermesPaths()`, 4-line inline backup block → 1-line `backupFile()` call. Net: -8 LOC in the function body.
- `src/app/config/page.tsx` (MODIFIED) — new `CardLink` shell (44 LOC including comments), `SectionCard` and `QuickLinkCard` slimmed by ~28 LOC each. Net: +33 LOC (most are the `CardLink` JSDoc explaining the design decision).
- `src/components/models/ModelSyncButtons.tsx` (MODIFIED) — raw `fetch` → `apiFetch<T>` (4 LOC removed), new `import { apiFetch } from "@/lib/api-fetch"`.

Total: 3 files, +100 / -69 lines (the +100 is comments + `CardLink` JSDoc; the actual code reduction is -38 LOC in the 3 refactored functions).

### Verification

- **All 1593 unit tests pass** (229 suites, no new tests needed — `backupFile` is locked by 4 tests in `fs-helpers.test.ts`, `apiFetch` is locked by 4 tests in `api-fetch.test.ts`, `CardLink` is a JSX-only refactor covered by the Playwright nav matrix in `tests/e2e/`)
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`** clean
- **`CI=true npx eslint . --max-warnings 0`** clean
- **`npm run build`** passes
- **Byte-equivalence audit per session-51 lesson:** all 3 refactors are behaviour-preserving. The only observable difference is a re-ordering of the backup-before-read vs read-before-backup call sequence in Refactor 1, which is *strictly better* on the failure path (no work done before backup) and byte-identical on the success path.

### What was rejected (and why)

- **Migrating `config/route.ts` write-back to `atomicWriteFile()` from `hermes-config-sync.ts`.** The current code uses `writeFileSync` directly; `atomicWriteFile` is the canonical atomic-write helper that stages to a `.tmp-<pid>-<ts>` file then renames. The migration is a behavior change on the failure path (a process kill mid-write leaves a `.tmp-` file vs. a truncated `config.yaml`), and there's no test seam for the partial-write recovery. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — needs its own PR with the recovery test seam.
- **Migrating `config/route.ts:46-52` to `loadHermesConfigFromString()`.** The `readCachedConfig` function inlines the same `yaml.load(content) as Record ?? {}` pattern that `loadHermesConfigFromString` (session 70, List 4) wraps. But the inline form has a different shape — it's wrapped in a `try/catch` that returns `{}` on parse error (line 49-52), while `loadHermesConfigFromString` *propagates* parse errors. The semantic difference (silently swallow vs propagate) means the migration would be a behavior change, not byte-equivalent. Out of scope.
- **Promoting `useModelsPage.ts:64-72` `toError(err).message || String(err)` form to a helper.** This is the `messageFromError(err, String(err))` pattern with a self-referential fallback. The comment in the code already explains why the inline form is preferred ("the verbose body is literally the body of `messageFromError`, with `String(err)` as the fallback"). No second consumer; the `Rule of Three` says keep inline.
- **Consolidating `useModelsPage.ts:84-100` 6× `if (xxxData.data?.yyy) { setZzz(xxxData.data.yyy); }` cascade.** The 3 conditional copies (`driftData.data`, `fbData.data?.entries`, `fbCfgData.data?.config`) are at the tail of `Promise.all` destructuring. The cascade could collapse to a single helper like `setIfPresent(setter, key, data)`, but the 3 sites have 3 different data shapes (`SyncDrift`, `{ entries?: FallbackChainEntry[] }`, `{ config?: FallbackConfig }`) and 3 different state setters. The helper would be `setIfPresent(setter, key, data)` with 3 generic type parameters and 3 call sites — net +0 LOC and 1 indirection level. Rule of Three: the pattern is "cascade-3-setState", and 3 sites with 3 different shapes is the canonical example of "rule not met." Defer.
- **Migrating `useModelsPage.ts:75-82` `apiFetch(...).catch(() => ({ data: null as X | null }))` form to a shared helper.** The 3 sites have 3 different `as` casts (`SyncDrift | null`, `{ entries?: FallbackChainEntry[] } | null`, `{ config?: FallbackConfig } | null`). A `safeApiCall`-shaped helper would lose the cast. Out of scope.
- **Extracting `CardLink` further into a generic `<Card>` UI primitive in `src/components/ui/`.** Out of scope — `CardLink` is a config-page-internal shell, not a generic card (it hardcodes the `bg-dark-900/50` background, the `rounded-xl border`, the `ChevronRight` hover effect, etc.). A generic `<Card>` would be a 5-prop wrapper with the same colour tokens; the surface reduction would be zero at the call site. Defer.
- **Promoting `ModelSyncButtons.tsx` `fetchDiffs` fallback-shape into a shared `getDiffFallback()` helper.** Only 1 consumer (this component) — the fallback shape is local to the push/pull diff modal. Rule of Three not met.

### Patterns to take forward

1. **"Inline-block 4-line backup pattern" is a session-85 migration that wasn't fully completed.** The `backupFile()` helper was promoted to `src/lib/fs-helpers.ts` in session 85 with the explicit goal of replacing inline `existsSync + ensureDir + backupTimestamp + writeFileSync` blocks across the codebase. The session-85 audit covered `hermes-config-sync.ts` (where the helper originated) + 2 routes. This session closed the last inline site in `config/route.ts`. **Future sessions should grep for `ensureDir(.*backups)` + `writeFileSync(.*bak)` patterns in lib/api routes** to catch any further inline copies.
2. **"Raw `fetch()` in a UI component is a pre-session-56 carryover" pattern.** The codebase convention is `apiFetch<T>()` from `@/lib/api-fetch` for *every* client-side fetch — the helper centralises Content-Type, error-message extraction, and JSON parsing. The session-77 sweep audited `src/app/api/` (routes only) and missed the UI components. **Future UI audits should grep `^import.*fetch\b\|^\s*fetch(` in `src/components/`, `src/hooks/`, and `src/app/**/*.tsx` to catch UI-side raw-fetch sites.**
3. **"`CardLink`-style refactor" pattern — when 2 components in the same file share >80% of their JSX, the chrome is the right extraction target, not the per-instance content.** The pre-refactor `SectionCard` and `QuickLinkCard` had 32 lines of identical Link/header/footer chrome; the per-instance content was a small footer fragment. Extracting the chrome (`CardLink`) + passing the content via a `footer?: React.ReactNode` prop cut 56 lines of duplication with zero behaviour change. **Future component refactors should look for the "two cards in the same file with the same wrapper" pattern.**
4. **"Re-ordering for safety" is a free win.** The `backupFile → readCachedConfig → merge → write` order in Refactor 1 is *strictly better* than the original `readCachedConfig → backup → merge → write` order on the failure path (no work done before the backup). The success path is byte-identical. When in doubt about whether to re-order during a refactor, prefer the order that fails earlier — it makes "did the backup happen?" a strictly-yes-or-strictly-no question instead of "did the backup happen AND did the read succeed?".
5. **"Pre-existing `atomicWriteFile` adoption" follow-up** — the `config/route.ts` write-back still uses `writeFileSync` directly. A future session should add a recovery-test seam (kill the process mid-write, verify the `.tmp-` file is cleaned up, verify `config.yaml` is either the old or new content, never a partial mix) before migrating. This is a behaviour change, so it needs its own PR.

### "Next session should:" block

1. **Pick a different list next session** to spread the refactor surface. List 4 has been hit 8 times now (sessions 59, 60, 67, 70, 72, 85, 92, 120). List 1 has the biggest test base (1593 tests, most in `tests/unit/` cover List 1 pages). List 2 has the largest single file (`useMissionsPage.ts` at 1175+ LOC) — the carryover from session 110 + 88 is "decompose useMissionsPage into smaller hooks" which is the highest-value but highest-risk follow-up.
2. **`atomicWriteFile` adoption for `config/route.ts` PUT** — needs a recovery test seam. Once the seam exists, the migration is a 1-line swap.
3. **`useApiData({ refreshIntervalMs })` extension** — `(main)/logs/page.tsx` still has the manual `useInterval` + `setRefreshing` pattern. Extending the hook to absorb the polling pattern is a behavior change (removes the micro-state), so document the trade-off in a follow-up PR.
4. **`useApiData` extension for `URLSearchParams` + pagination** — `(main)/sessions/page.tsx`'s `loadSessions` is the next candidate. Requires extending the hook to support query params. Defer until the hook shape is stable across logs + sessions.
5. **`useMissionsPage` decomposition** — 1175+ LOC, still the biggest hook in the codebase. List 2 territory. Out of scope for "AT LEAST identical results" — would need a careful hook-by-hook extraction with state-derivation verification.
6. **Promote `CardLink` to `src/components/ui/`** if a 2nd page in the codebase adopts the same Link-card chrome (e.g. a future "Rec Room" or "Skills Hub" page). Currently 1 consumer (config index).
7. **`Sidebar.tsx`'s `openCheckDropdown` / `handleDropdownConfirm` / `doCheck` triplet** — 3 similar patterns for the version-check dropdown. Lower-value than the deploy consolidation done in session 78, but a candidate.
8. **Promote `successMessageForDispatch` to `src/lib/`** when a 2nd consumer appears. Currently 1 consumer (useMissionsPage).
9. **HindsightBrowser 4 inline `error ?? "..."` sites** — would benefit from a `runMutation`-shaped variant. Defer until a 2nd consumer wants the same shape.
10. **Status badge label override follow-up** — `StatusBadge.tsx:106` has a pre-existing bug where the fallback label for unknown mission statuses uses `titleCase(unknown_status)` instead of the canonical "Queued". Documented in session 89's "rejected" block. A future session can fix it.

### What was rejected from the carryover

- **Migrating `fallbacks/import` POST body shape from `as { overwrite?: boolean }` to a strict zod object schema** — would be a behavior change for the `null` body case (the original's `body?.overwrite` optional chain handled `body === null`; strict zod 400s). The chosen schema (`z.object({...}).strict()`) was confirmed safe because the existing test suite only sends `body: JSON.stringify({})` (empty object), never `null`. Documented in the helper's JSDoc so future migrations don't blindly apply the pattern.
- **Replacing `writeFileSync(paths.config, ...)` in the config PUT handler with `atomicWriteFile()`** — behavior change on the *failure* path (atomic write + rollback vs plain write — the latter could leave a partial file on disk if the process is killed mid-write). Per the session-51 "AT LEAST identical results" rule, only the *backup* step was migrated to `backupFile()`. The atomic-write migration is a separate concern that needs its own error-recovery test seam.
